  7. Configuration tree (config, 1084 files, complete by family)
  - config/dtd/AnaWSBuilder.dtd: Canonical DTD schema used by XML workspace configs.
  - All files in config/bbyy (4 files): Run-1 diphoton XML templates/configuration definitions.
  - All files in config/high_mass_diphoton/graviton/2015 and config/high_mass_diphoton/graviton/2016
  (8 files) plus parents (2 files): Graviton signal/background/category configs for high-mass
  diphoton runs.
  - All files in config/ttHyy and config/ttHyy/model (25 files): ttHyy category/background/signal
  templates and concrete XMLs.
  - All files in config/dijetisrTLA (22 files): Main ISR-TLA analysis templates for
  top/category/background and baseline XMLs.
  - All files in config/dijetisrTLA/signal (75 files), config/dijetisrTLA/forTomas/signal (48
  files): Signal XML grids over mean/width points.
  - All files in config/dijetisrTLA/noSyst (4 files): No-systematics template variant.
  - All files in config/dijetTLA (40 files), config/dijetTLA/20200630_j75_bkgonly_test (4),
  config/dijetTLA/20200701_j75_bkgonly_test (5): Dijet TLA setup and archived test campaign configs.
  - All files in config/dijetTLA/signal (293 files): Dijet TLA signal scan XMLs over mass/width or
  resonance hypotheses.
  - All files in config/dijetTLAnlo (11 files): NLO top/category/background base templates/XMLs.
  - All files in config/dijetTLAnlo/signal (221 files), config/dijetTLAnlo/signal/old (90 files):
  NLO signal workspace XML grids and older generations.
  - All files in config/dijetTLAnlo/templates2021 (6 files): 2021 template set for regenerated
  configs.
  - All files in config/dijetTLAnlo/templates2021/signal (222 files): 2021 generated signal XML scan
  grid.
  - In short: every XML/template file under config is either top-level analysis wiring, category
  definition, background parameterization, or one signal mass-width grid point.

  **Quick Setup**
  - Prepare environment (this mirrors the example script which sources

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/scripts/setup_buildAndFit.sh]

  ):
  ```bash
  # from repo root
  source scripts/setup_buildAndFit.sh
  ```
  - If you need BumpHunter, install pyBumpHunter:
  ```bash
  bash scripts/install_pyBumpHunter.sh
  ```

  **Example: run a single B-only fit and produce post-fit plots**
  - Edit or set `OUT_DIR` (example uses `./out`), then run the same command used in

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/scripts/run_anaFit.sh]

   (fields shown with example values):
  ```bash
  export OUT_DIR=./out
  mkdir -p $OUT_DIR/run_135_1000_sevenPar
  FOLDER=$OUT_DIR/run_135_1000_sevenPar

  python3 python/run_anaFit.py \
    --datafile data/data23_histos.root \
    --datahist mjj \
    --backgroundfile config/dijetisrTLA/background_dijetisrTLA_sevenPar.template \
    --signalfile config/dijetisrTLA/signal/signal_dijetisrTLA.template \
    --categoryfile config/dijetisrTLA/category_dijetisrTLA.template \
    --topfile config/dijetisrTLA/dijetisrTLA.template \
    --wsfile ${FOLDER}/dijetisrTLA_combWS_sevenPar.root \
    --sigmean 400 \
    --sigwidth 8 \
    --nbkg dummy \
    --rangelow 135 \
    --rangehigh 1000 \
    --outputfile ${FOLDER}/FitResult_anaFit_sevenPar_bkgOnly.root \
    --maskthreshold 0.01 \
    --folder ${FOLDER} \
    --doprefit
  ```
  - Make the standard post‑fit plot (script uses this exact command):
  ```bash
  python3 python/plotPostFit.py -i ${FOLDER}/PostFit_anaFit_sevenPar_bkgOnly.root -o
  ${FOLDER}/postFit.pdf
  ```
  - Also run the ROOT macro that makes the paper-style plots:
  ```bash
  root -l -q "plot_postfit.cpp(\"${FOLDER}\", \"seven\")"
  ```

  **Chi2 / stability checks (get p(χ2) and stability)**
  - Create the chi2 distribution from many toys / fits and fit it:
  ```bash
  python3 python/getChi2Distribution.py --infiles <toy1.root> <toy2.root> ... --inhist chi2
  --outfile ${FOLDER}/chi2.root
  # view and save png/pdf:
  # the script writes ${FOLDER}/chi2.png and chi2.pdf
  ```
  - Visualise ndof vs p-value:
  ```bash
  python3 python/plotChi2Ndof.py --infiles ${FOLDER}/some_chi2_files.root --outfile
  ${FOLDER}/pvals.root
  ```
  (see

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/python/getChi2Distribution.py]

   and

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/python/plotChi2Ndof.py]

   for argument details)

  **BumpHunter window exclusion (BH window)**
  - Example usage (matches how

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/python/run_anaFit.py]

   calls it): activate the pyBumpHunter venv then run

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/python/FindBHWindow.py]

   on the postfit file:
  ```bash
  # activate the pyBumpHunter env (created by scripts/install_pyBumpHunter.sh)
  source pyBumpHunter/pyBH_env/bin/activate

  # run BH on the postfit histograms produced by run_anaFit
  python3 python/FindBHWindow.py \
    --inputfile ${FOLDER}/PostFit_anaFit_sevenPar_bkgOnly.root \
    --bkghist Run3TLA_rebinned/postfit \
    --datahist Run3TLA_rebinned/data \
    --outputjson ${FOLDER}/BHresults.json

  deactivate
  ```

    +## Outputs To Inspect
    +
    +If your current interest is only the data-inspection part of the workflow, these are the

    +
    +- `<output-folder>/FitResult_anaFit_*.root` is the main fit-result ROOT file written by
    [python/run_anaFit.py](python/run_anaFit.py). It stores the fit summary, nuisance-parameter
    information, and the fit quality values used to decide whether the fit passes.
    +- `<output-folder>/quickFitLog_*.log` is the solver log from `quickFit`. If the fit behaves
    badly or fails to converge, this is the first place to look.
    +- `<output-folder>/edm_*.pdf` is the convergence plot generated from the `quickFit` log by
    [plot_edm.py](plot_edm.py). It helps diagnose whether the minimizer reached a stable point.
    +- `<output-folder>/PostFit_anaFit_*.root` is the post-fit histogram output produced by
    [python/ExtractPostfitFromWS.py](python/ExtractPostfitFromWS.py). This is the file that feeds
    the post-fit plots, residual checks, and BumpHunter masking step.
    +- `<output-folder>/postFit.pdf` is the compact post-fit comparison from
    [python/plotPostFit.py](python/plotPostFit.py).
    +- `<output-folder>/BHresults.json` is the BumpHunter summary from
    [python/FindBHWindow.py](python/FindBHWindow.py). It contains `MaskMin`, `MaskMax`, and
    `BlindRange`, which tell you what to exclude if the fit is not stable.
    +- `<output-folder>/*_masked.root` and `<output-folder>/*_masked.xml` are the masked rerun

  will cloneing the repo inside of the main repo at the top level work?

[2026-07-06 14:22:53] ASSISTANT (copilot/auto):
  Yes. Cloning those repos directly into the root of FrequentistFramework will work, because the
  setup scripts and analysis code expect sibling directories named `xmlAnaWSBuilder`, `quickFit`,
  `workspaceCombiner`, and

  .

  A few caveats matter:
  - They should be cloned as top-level folders, not inside another subdirectory.
  - The main repo will then show those directories as untracked unless they are ignored or already
  managed as submodules.
  - On Windows, long paths can bite, so keeping the workspace path short helps.
  - Use the pinned commits from

[reference: /c:/Users/harry/OneDrive - The University of Manchester/Documents/work/Atlas/FrequentistFramework/install.sh]

   if you want the scripts to behave consistently.

  So the short answer is: yes, top-level cloning is the right layout for local use.

  Yes — if the SSH repo is a separate clone, the safest flow is:

  1. Commit and push from SSH
  2. Pull from local

  So the local copy does not need the changes first. The local repo only needs to receive them via
  pull.

  If you want the most conservative workflow, use this order:

  - SSH:
    - stage
    - commit
    - push

  - Local:
    - pull

  be pulled before you push anything.
